--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The mission is AGGREGATION (robots should stay in the target region - the BLACK strip bordered by BLUE walls). The score increases for every robot outside the target region.

**Key observations from Round 1:**
- Best machine (score 17002): `GoAwayColor` with color=2 (green). This makes sense! Robots move AWAY from green walls, pushing them toward the blue-walled target strip.
- 2nd best (18712): Simple repulsion - robots spread but don't necessarily reach target
- 3rd best (18880): Complex 3-state with repulsion involved
- The champion is very simple: flee from green (color 2) → end up at blue strip

**Strategy:**
1. Keep the champion (GoAwayColor, clr=2)
2. Exploit: Try GoToColor with blue (color 3) - robots move TOWARD blue walls
3. Try 2-state machines: GoAwayColor from green as base, with transitions to attraction or stopping when on black floor
4. Try GoToColor clr=3 (blue walls) variations
5. Mix: explore on gray, go to blue on black (already in target)

**State designs:**
- GoAwayColor(green) is the champion - exploit this
- GoToColor(blue) should also work well
- 2-state: explore/GoAwayColor + transition on black floor (already in target → stop or attract)
- 3-state with floor detection: if gray→GoAwayColor(green), if black→stop/attract

Let me design 14 machines:

1. **CHAMPION** (exact copy)
2. GoToColor blue (clr=3) - direct approach
3. GoToColor blue (clr=3) different LED
4. 2-state: GoAwayColor(green) + stop when on black floor
5. 2-state: GoAwayColor(green) + attract when on black floor  
6. 2-state: GoToColor(blue) + stop when on black floor
7. 2-state: GoAwayColor(green) base, transition to GoToColor(blue) on gray
8. 3-state: explore → GoAwayColor(green) → stop on black
9. GoAwayColor green with different parameters
10. 2-state: GoToColor(blue) + GoAwayColor(green) with floor transitions
11. 3-state: GoToColor(blue) + stop on black + exploration backup
12. GoAwayColor(green) + repulsion 2-state
13. 2-state: GoAwayColor green ↔ GoToColor blue with neighbor conditions
14. 3-state with floor-based navigation

**Key insights from Round 2:**

Massive improvement! The champion scores 3502.67, using a 2-state machine:
- State 0: GoAwayColor(green=2) → transitions to state 1 if on BLACK floor (p=0.85) OR if sees blue (color 3) (p=0.7)
- State 1: GoToColor(blue=3) → transitions back to state 0 if on GRAY floor (p=0.7)

This is elegant: flee green until black floor detected, then go toward blue. The combination is powerful.

2nd best (3630): Simple GoToColor(blue) alone. Very good but slightly worse.

There's a huge gap between rank 2 (3630) and rank 3 (8556). So the top 2 approaches are clearly superior.

**Strategy for Round 3:**
1. Keep the champion exactly
2. Exploit champion topology: vary probabilities (0.85→0.9, 0.7→0.75, etc.)
3. Try 2-state with GoToColor(blue) as primary + GoAwayColor(green) as backup
4. Try 3-state refinements of the champion pattern
5. Try variations with stop state when on black floor (already in target)
6. Explore: GoToColor(blue) with neighbor-based transitions

Note the error: missing destination index in previous answer. Must be careful with `--n<I>x<K>` format.

**Machines:**
1. Champion (exact copy)
2. Exploit: p0x0=0.9, p0x1=0.75, p1x0=0.65
3. Exploit: p0x0=0.8, p0x1=0.75, p1x0=0.75
4. Exploit: p0x0=0.9, p0x1=0.65, p1x0=0.7
5. Exploit: add stop state when enough robots near (aggregation detected)
6. GoToColor(blue) base + GoAwayColor(green) when on gray
7. 3-state: GoAwayColor(green) → GoToColor(blue) → stop on black
8. GoToColor(blue) + transition back to explore on gray
9. Champion topology but with neighbor condition added
10. 2-state: GoToColor(blue) primary, GoAwayColor(green) when on gray, return on black
11. 3-state with stop when on black (high neighbor count in target)
12. Vary color stop transition probability
13. GoToColor(blue) alone (keep 2nd best)
14. 2-state with attraction instead of GoToColor in state 1

## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 3:**

The top 7 machines all score between 3328-3416, using the same 2-state topology:
- State 0: GoAwayColor(green=2) with two transitions: on BLACK floor (p≈0.85-0.95) → state 1, AND sees blue color (p≈0.65-0.8) → state 1
- State 1: GoToColor(blue=3) with one transition: on GRAY floor (p≈0.6-0.75) → state 0

The champion has p0x0=0.95, p0x1=0.8, p1x0=0.6.

The scores are very close (3328-3416), suggesting we're near a local optimum with this topology. The gap to rank 8 (3533 - simple GoToColor) is meaningful.

**What to try:**
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Fine-tune: try p0x0 values 0.95-1.0, p0x1 values 0.8-0.9, p1x0 values 0.5-0.65
3. Try adding a 3rd transition to state 1 (e.g., stay if many neighbors = already aggregated)
4. Try adding stop state for when robots are well-aggregated (on black with many neighbors)
5. Explore: 3-state with stop when on black AND many neighbors
6. Try lower p1x0 (less likely to leave GoToColor when on gray) - makes robots persist toward blue
7. Try higher p0x0 (very likely to switch to GoToColor when on black)

The key parameters seem to be: high p0x0 (switch quickly when on black), high p0x1 (switch when seeing blue), moderate p1x0 (return to flee-green when straying to gray).

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 4:**

The top machines continue to converge on the same 2-state topology. The champion has:
- p0x0 = 0.95 (switch to GoToColor when on black floor)
- p0x1 = 0.90 (switch to GoToColor when sees blue)
- p1x0 = 0.50 (switch back to GoAwayColor when on gray)

The scores are very tight (3304-3395 for top 7), all within ~3% of each other. The 3-state machines crashed badly (11508), suggesting the 2-state topology is clearly superior here.

Key observations:
- Higher p0x1 (0.9) seems better than 0.8 - quickly switch to GoToColor when blue is visible
- Lower p1x0 (0.5) seems better - don't rush back to flee-green once heading toward blue
- p0x0 around 0.95-0.98 seems optimal

**Error note:** "Transition '--n1x1' is missing its destination index" - I must write `--n1x1 D` with the destination D. Looking at my previous answers, in the 3-state machines I wrote `--n1x1 0 --c1x1 1` etc. which should be correct. The issue might be in some 2-state entries. Let me be extra careful.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Fine-tune p0x1 (try 0.85-0.95), p1x0 (try 0.4-0.55), p0x0 (try 0.93-0.98)
3. Try p0x1=1.0 (always switch when seeing blue)
4. Try adding a second condition to state 1 (e.g., sees green → back to flee)
5. Keep GoToColor(blue) alone as backup
6. Explore slightly different parameter combinations

## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 5:**

The new champion is a reversed topology! Instead of GoAwayColor(green) → GoToColor(blue), it's:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) → transitions to state 1 if on GRAY floor (p=0.5)
- State 1: GoAwayColor(green=2) → transitions to state 0 if on BLACK floor (p=0.95) OR sees blue (p=0.9)

This is essentially the same logic but starting from GoToColor(blue). Score: 3268.54, slightly better.

The scores are still very tight (3268-3397 for top 13, with big gap to 3526 for single-state).

**What to exploit:**
- The champion's p0x0=0.5 (leave GoToColor when on gray) could be varied
- p1x0=0.95 (switch from flee-green to go-blue when on black) - try higher/lower
- p1x1=0.9 (switch when sees blue) - try higher/lower
- Try adding a second transition to state 0 (e.g., sees green → stay in flee state)
- The inverted topology (GoToColor first) seems slightly better

**Error note:** "--n1x1 is missing its destination index" - I need to make sure `--n1x1 D` includes D. Looking carefully at my outputs, in the 2-state machine `--n1x1 0` means destination=0 which targets state 0 (the only other state). This seems correct. Let me verify the format once more and be very careful.

**Machines to generate:**
1. Champion (exact copy)
2. Vary p0x0 around 0.5 (0.4, 0.45, 0.55, 0.6)
3. Vary p1x0 around 0.95 (0.9, 0.98, 1.0)
4. Vary p1x1 around 0.9 (0.85, 0.95, 1.0)
5. Add second transition to state 0 (sees green → go to flee)
6. Try adding sees-blue trigger to state 0 transitions
7. Keep previous best (GoAwayColor first, p0x0=0.95, p0x1=0.9, p1x0=0.4)

## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 6:**

The champion has p0x0=0.4 (lower = better for switching from GoToColor to GoAwayColor when on gray). The trend is clear: lower p0x0 is better. The scores tightly cluster between 3185-3332.

Key parameter trends:
- p0x0: lower is better (0.4 < 0.45 < 0.5 < 0.55 < 0.6). Try 0.3, 0.35
- p1x0 (black floor → switch to GoToColor): 0.95 seems best, try 0.93, 0.97
- p1x1 (sees blue → switch to GoToColor): 0.9 seems good, 0.85-0.95 all competitive

The topology is locked in: GoToColor(blue) → [gray floor p0x0] → GoAwayColor(green) → [black floor p1x0, sees blue p1x1] → GoToColor(blue)

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Explore lower p0x0: try 0.3, 0.35, 0.25
3. Fine-tune p1x0 around 0.95 with p0x0=0.4
4. Fine-tune p1x1 around 0.9 with p0x0=0.4
5. Try combining p0x0=0.35-0.4 with various p1x0 and p1x1 combinations
6. One exploration: add sees-green trigger to state 0 to reinforce fleeing

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 7:**

The champion (3185.95) still holds from round 6. The best new machine this round scored 3239.16 with p0x0=0.25. The trend continues: lower p0x0 seems better (0.25 < 0.30 < 0.35 < 0.40).

The fixed structure is:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3), leave when on GRAY floor (p0x0)
- State 1: GoAwayColor(green=2), switch to GoToColor when on BLACK (p1x0=0.95) or sees blue (p1x1=0.9)

Lower p0x0 means robots spend less time fleeing back to GoAwayColor when they wander onto gray - they persist in GoToColor(blue) mode longer. This makes sense: once heading toward blue, keep going.

**Parameter exploration:**
- p0x0: try 0.2, 0.15, 0.1 (going lower)
- p1x0: 0.95 seems optimal, try 0.95 with lower p0x0
- p1x1: 0.9 seems good, try 0.85-0.9 with lower p0x0

Note: The error about "--n1x1 missing destination" keeps appearing. I'm writing `--n1x1 0` which should be correct. Let me re-examine: in 2-state FSM leaving state 1, destination D=0 means the other state (state 0). This should be correct per the rules. Perhaps the issue is in 3-state machines. I'll avoid 3-state machines this round.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 8:**

Champion: p0x0=0.1, score=3107.52. Second best: p0x0=0.05, score=3121.43. The trend is very clear - lower p0x0 is better, meaning robots should rarely leave GoToColor(blue) when on gray floor. Try p0x0=0.0 (never leave) and values between 0 and 0.1.

But wait - p0x0=0.0 would mean robots NEVER transition from state 0 to state 1, making state 1 unreachable. That's equivalent to just GoToColor(blue) alone, which scored ~3440-3530. So there must be an optimal low value.

The optimal range seems to be 0.05-0.1. Let me try: 0.05, 0.07, 0.1, 0.08, 0.12.

Also: p1x0=0.95 and p1x1=0.9 seem solid. p1x0=0.98 was rank 3 with p0x0=0.2, suggesting higher p1x0 might help with very low p0x0.

**Strategy:**
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Fine-tune p0x0 around 0.05-0.12
3. Try p0x0=0.05 with varied p1x0 (0.95, 0.98, 1.0) and p1x1 (0.85, 0.9)
4. Try p0x0=0.1 with varied p1x0 and p1x1
5. One exploration: try p0x0 very small (0.02, 0.03) to see if trend continues

**About the error:** The persistent "--n1x1 missing destination" error is puzzling since I do write `--n1x1 0`. I wonder if it's a parsing issue with the simulator when the destination comes right before `--c1x1`. Let me try the exact same format as the champion which works: `--n1x1 0 --c1x1 7 --p1x1 0.9 --l1x1 3`.

## Reasoning

**Key insights from Round 9:**

Champion: p0x0=0.03, score=3049.52. The sweet spot for p0x0 is around 0.03-0.07. Notably:
- p0x0=0.02 scored 3153 (worse than 0.03)
- p0x0=0.03 scored 3049 (best)
- p0x0=0.05 scored 3059 (close second)
- p0x0=0.07 scored 3079

So there's a clear optimum around 0.03-0.05. Going too low (0.02) hurts because robots never return to GoAwayColor(green) mode when needed.

For p1x0: 0.95, 0.98, 1.0 all competitive with p0x0=0.05. The differences are small.
For p1x1: 0.9 seems better than 0.85.

**This is the final round.** I should:
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Fine-tune p0x0 around 0.03-0.05 (try 0.025, 0.03, 0.04, 0.05)
3. Try p1x0=0.98, 1.0 with p0x0=0.03
4. Try p1x1=0.92, 0.95 with p0x0=0.03
5. Combine best parameters: p0x0=0.03-0.05, p1x0=0.95-0.98, p1x1=0.9
6. One exploration with slightly different structure

The champion format works, so I'll replicate it carefully. The `--n1x1 0` destination issue - I notice the champion itself uses `--n1x1 0` and it works fine (it's the best machine). The error must come from some other machines I submitted. I'll stick strictly to the champion's topology.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
